            // Servitor/Kasserer -> Standard, Leder/Eier -> Godkjenner.
            migrationBuilder.Sql(

            migrationBuilder.Sql(
                "UPDATE [Operators] SET [RoleLevel] = 'Godkjenner' WHERE [RoleLevel] IN ('Leder', 'Eier');");
        }

        /// <inheritdoc />
        protected override void Down(MigrationBuilder migrationBuilder)
        {
            // Best-effort reverse of the role collapse (lossy: the original four titles cannot be
            // recovered). Map back to a representative title so the old enum still deserializes.
            migrationBuilder.Sql(
                "UPDATE [Operators] SET [RoleLevel] = 'Leder' WHERE [RoleLevel] = 'Godkjenner';");
            migrationBuilder.Sql(
                "UPDATE [Operators] SET [RoleLevel] = 'Servitor' WHERE [RoleLevel] = 'Standard';");

            // ---- Physical invariants: filtered unique index + append-only/immutability triggers ----
            // Layer 2 of the two-layer defence the module specs describe; layer 1 is GuardAppendOnly in
            // ApplicationDbContext. Copied VERBATIM from the source branch's per-wave migrations, whose
            // own comments instruct that these blocks be re-applied by hand whenever this migration is
            // regenerated -- `dotnet ef migrations add` scaffolds from the model and cannot reproduce raw
            // SQL, which is exactly how they were lost in the port. Message text and THROW numbers are
            // asserted by the lineage tests, so neither may be reworded. No model configuration
            // accompanies any of this, deliberately: that is what keeps HasPendingModelChanges() clean.
            // The Kassa journal triggers from the source M0 migration are NOT ported -- feature/POS has
            // never carried any trigger, so that is inherited POS state and a POS-owner decision.

            // D1 (spec 3.1 / 4.2): at most ONE active engagement per legal employer, regardless of store.
            // A filtered unique index so a race can never create a second active engagement for the same
            // (person, legal employer). Deliberately a migration operation with NO OnModelCreating
            // counterpart -- the same discipline the triggers use -- which is why a snapshot diff cannot
            // see it, and why the port lost it. The service discriminates on this index NAME to map its
            // unique violation to the typed 409, so the name is load-bearing.
            migrationBuilder.CreateIndex(
                name: "UX_WorkforceStaffMembers_ActiveEngagement",
                table: "WorkforceStaffMembers",

                unique: true,

            migrationBuilder.Sql(@"CREATE TRIGGER [dbo].[TR_TrainingCourseVersions_ImmutableAfterPublish]
ON [dbo].[TrainingCourseVersions]
AFTER UPDATE, DELETE
AS
BEGIN
    SET NOCOUNT ON;

    -- DELETE branch (no inserted rows): a Published or Retired version can never be deleted.
    IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM inserted)
    BEGIN
        IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM deleted WHERE [State] IN ('Published', 'Retired'))
        BEGIN
            ROLLBACK TRANSACTION;
            THROW 50053, 'TrainingCourseVersions is immutable once Published: a Published or Retired course version cannot be deleted; a change is a new version.', 1;
        END
        RETURN;
    END

    -- UPDATE branch: any OLD row already Published/Retired is frozen, EXCEPT the single permitted
    -- pure transition Published -> Retired with every content column unchanged.
    IF EXISTS (
        SELECT 1
        FROM deleted d
        INNER JOIN inserted i ON i.CourseVersionId = d.CourseVersionId
        WHERE d.[State] IN ('Published', 'Retired')
          AND NOT (
                d.[State] = 'Published' AND i.[State] = 'Retired'
                AND NOT EXISTS (
                    SELECT d.VersionNo, d.CourseId, d.StoreId, d.ContentPagesJson, d.QuizJson, d.PassThresholdPercent, d.ContentHash
                    EXCEPT
                    SELECT i.VersionNo, i.CourseId, i.StoreId, i.ContentPagesJson, i.QuizJson, i.PassThresholdPercent, i.ContentHash
                )
          )
    )
    BEGIN
        ROLLBACK TRANSACTION;
        THROW 50053, 'TrainingCourseVersions is immutable once Published: the only permitted change to a Published version is the pure Published->Retired transition with content unchanged; a content edit, a Published->Draft demotion, or any Retired-row edit is a new version.', 1;
    END
END");
        }

            // and there is deliberately NO backfill statement. Both halves matter.
            //
            // EventsPaymentReceipts is append-only in TWO layers — GuardAppendOnly in ApplicationDbContext
            // and TR_EventsPaymentReceipts_AppendOnly, an AFTER UPDATE, DELETE trigger that ROLLBACKs the
            // transaction and THROWs 50022. A backfill `UPDATE EventsPaymentReceipts SET ActorKind = ...`
            // would therefore roll this whole migration back on any database that already holds a receipt.
            // ALTER TABLE ... ADD is DDL and fires no DML trigger, so the column default is the only route
            // that can reach an existing row at all.
            //
            // "System" is also the truthful value rather than a value picked to satisfy the constraint.
            // Every receipt written before this column existed came from the completion sink (a provider
            // webhook) or from an admin path that recorded no actor anywhere, so no existing row can name a
            // human. Defaulting to Admin would have manufactured one; defaulting to "" would have produced
            // exactly the row this lane exists to eliminate — a kind that says nothing paired with an actor
            // that names nobody. System with a NULL ActorUserId is coherent under the rule
            // EventsPaymentLedger enforces (Admin requires a non-blank actor; Guest/System require an
            // EXACTLY-null one), so no legacy row violates the invariant new rows are held to.
            migrationBuilder.AddColumn<string>(
                name: "ActorKind",
                table: "EventsPaymentReceipts",
                type: "nvarchar(16)",
                maxLength: 16,
                nullable: false,
                defaultValue: "System");

            migrationBuilder.AddColumn<string>(
                name: "ActorUserId",

                type: "nvarchar(128)",

@@FILE 20260730150953_Growth_ConsentTextVersionAppendOnly.cs
﻿using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Migrations;

#nullable disable

namespace WebApi.Migrations
{
    /// <inheritdoc />
    public partial class Growth_ConsentTextVersionAppendOnly : Migration
    {
        // The one append-only Growth family that had no layer-2 trigger. Its three siblings — consent
        // receipts, suppressions and consent-check receipts — each got one in RestaurantModules_Initial
        // (THROW 50030-50032, plus 50033 for the provider inbox); GrowthConsentTextVersions was grouped with
        // the MUTATING Growth tables by a comment in ApplicationDbContext and so got neither layer.
        //
        // It belongs with the ledger, not with them: spec §13 requires that every consent receipt pins the
        // consent-text version, and a receipt pins it BY ID as the evidence that consent was lawfully
        // obtained. Editing a version's wording therefore rewrites what an already-captured guest is
        // recorded as having agreed to, retroactively and invisibly — the receipt still verifies, and is
        // now wrong. There is nothing to correct afterwards, which is the whole reason the layer exists.
        //
        // Deliberately NO model configuration accompanies this: a raw trigger has no snapshot counterpart,
        // which is what keeps HasPendingModelChanges() clean (the discipline RestaurantModules_Initial's
        // own trigger block states). Layer 1 is the GuardAppendOnly entry landing in the same commit, so the
        // SQLite fast suite and SQL Server refuse the same write; this layer is what reaches a DBA session,
        // a report tool or any other writer that never passes through the DbContext.
        //
        // INSTEAD OF, not AFTER, and 50034: both match the Growth family. The message must contain
        // "append-only" — GrowthMigrationLineageTests asserts on that substring for every table it lists.
        protected override void Up(MigrationBuilder migrationBuilder)
        {
            migrationBuilder.Sql(@"CREATE TRIGGER [dbo].[TR_GrowthConsentTextVersions_AppendOnly]
ON [dbo].[GrowthConsentTextVersions]
INSTEAD OF UPDATE, DELETE
AS
BEGIN
    SET NOCOUNT ON;
    THROW 50034, 'GrowthConsentTextVersions is append-only: UPDATE and DELETE are not permitted (publish new wording as a new version).', 1;
END;");
        }

        /// <inheritdoc />
        protected override void Down(MigrationBuilder migrationBuilder)
        {
            // Dropped, not left behind: RestaurantModulesMigrationRoundTripTests rolls the whole module
            // surface back and re-applies it, and a trigger that survives its own Down fails the second Up
            // by already existing.
            migrationBuilder.Sql("DROP TRIGGER [dbo].[TR_GrowthConsentTextVersions_AppendOnly];");
        }
    }
}
